\setlength{\abovedisplayskip}{6pt plus 2pt minus 2pt}

\linespread{0.97}\selectfont
\section{Witness-modulus tails and the effectivity repair}
\label{sec:witness-tail}
For a prime, or any positive integer, $p$, define
\begin{equation}\tag{51.1}
 W(p)=W_{\rm II}(p)=\min k\ell,
\end{equation}
where the minimum is over positive $k,\ell,u,v,w$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\tag{51.2}
 k\ell\equiv3\pmod4,\qquad \frac{k\ell+1}{4}=uvw,
 \qquad p\equiv-uv^{-1}\pmod {k\ell}.
\end{equation}
Put $W(p)=+\infty$ if there is no such tuple; no general finiteness
assertion is made.  Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplier} says exactly that $W(p)\leq T$
supplies an explicit representation with multiplier modulus at most $T$.
The definition allows composite $\ell$, although the proof uses a prime
subfamily.

\subsection{Parameterized truncation and the master tail}
Write $t=\log X$, let $K\geq K_0$, and suppose that the canonical dyadic
boxes (39.2)--(39.3) furnish, uniformly in every
$\mathcal J\subseteq\{k\leq K:k\equiv1\pmod4\}$ containing $1$ and every
reduced compatible fibre $c$,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.3}
 \sum_{X^{1/2}<\ell\leq X}\frac{f_{c;\mathcal J}(\ell)}\ell
 \asymp t^2h(\mathcal J),\qquad
 h(\mathcal J)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal J}\frac{\varphi(k)}{k^2}.
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}\tag{51.4}
                         \mu=t^2\log K.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[truncated $c$-free assembly; proved internally, with the
provisional review status of the fixed-$c$-free chain]
\label{lem:truncated-cfree}
Assume (51.3), $KX\leq T$, $\log K\ll t$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{51.5}
                         \log N\geq C\mu t.
\end{equation}
Then, with effective constants whenever the supply constants in (51.3) are
effective,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.6}
 \#\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W(p)>T\}
       \ll N\exp\{-c\mu\}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use the atoms (39.2)--(39.4), all of modulus at most $KX\leq T$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:cfree}'s geometry needs only $u,v\leq X^{1/6}$ and
$4uv>4H^2>K$.  The profile and exact CRT/Euler induction replay as
\begin{equation}\tag{51.7--51.8}
 W_{k,a}(g)\ll\frac{t^2}{\varphi(g)}\frac{\varphi(k)^2}{k^3},
 \qquad \mathbb E(H_X)_m\leq(C\mu)^m.
\end{equation}
for every needed order.  With $y=B\mu$, the bad fibres cost
$\exp\{-\eta B\mu+O(1)\}$, while the others have
$h(\mathcal J_c)\gg\log K$; (51.3) therefore gives
$\Pr(H_X=0\mid S_y=1)\leq e^{-c\mu}$.  The fixed-polylog lower proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-harvest} and the cubic proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} both supply exactly these canonical dyadic boxes.

Take the least even $r\geq D_B\mu$.  The same Bonferroni factorial tail has
CRT mean $O(e^{-c\mu})$ and ledger
\begin{equation}\tag{51.11}\label{eq:witness-ledger}
 \deg=O(\mu),\qquad \log d_{\rm term}=O(\mu t),\qquad
 \log\sum|c_{\rm term}|=O(\mu t).
\end{equation}
Since $y<X^{1/2}$ and the atom count has logarithm $O(t)$, (51.5) absorbs
rounding and the primes at most $\max(K,y)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[master witness-modulus tail; proved internally with the
exact status below]\label{thm:witness-tail}
There are effectively computable positive constants such that the following
hold.  Every set counted with variable $p$ in this theorem and
Corollary~\ref{cor:witness-polylog} is restricted to primes.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,label=\arabic*.]
\item \textbf{Fixed-polylogarithmic supply, without
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.}  Uniformly when
\begin{equation}\tag{51.12}
 3\leq T,\qquad
 \log N\geq C\{1+(\log T)^3\log(2+\log T)\},
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\tag{51.13}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>T\}
 \ll N\exp\{-c(\log T)^2\log(2+\log T)\}.
\end{equation}
This is unconditional in the no-unproved-hypothesis sense and does not
inherit Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}; it does inherit the campaign's
provisional-review qualification on the Section~\ref{sec:cfree} moment and
Bonferroni machinery.
\item \textbf{Cubic supply.}  Fix $0<\kappa<1/240$.  Uniformly when
\begin{equation}\tag{51.14}
 3\leq T,\qquad \log N\geq C_\kappa\{1+(\log T)^4\},
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\tag{51.15}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>T\}
 \ll_\kappa N\exp\{-c_\kappa(\log T)^3\}.
\end{equation}
This statement inherits Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} and is
\textsc{claimed/provisional}.
\end{enumerate}
Both estimates are uniform throughout the simpler common range
\begin{equation}\tag{51.16}
                    3\leq T\leq\exp\{c(\log N)^{1/4}\}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For (1), set $X=T^{1/2}$ and
$K=\lfloor(\log X)^5\rfloor$, with fixed adjustments below the asymptotic
threshold.  Then $KX\leq T$, $\log K\asymp\log t$, and the canonical-box
proof of \eqref{eq:mult-mass} supplies (51.3).  Thus
$\mu\asymp t^2\log t$ and (51.5) is (51.12).  For (2), use the same $X$
and $K=\lfloor X^\kappa\rfloor$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} supplies (51.3),
$\mu\asymp t^3$, and (51.5) becomes (51.14).  Bounded $T$ is absorbed by
enlarging the implied constants.
\end{proof}
At $T=\exp\{\alpha(\log N)^{1/4}\}$, (51.15) is
$N\exp\{-c(\log N)^{3/4}\}$, exactly the prime part of (39.35) up to
constants.  This is a consistency check, not a new record.

\begin{corollary}[polylogarithmic tails; same status as
Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-tail}]\label{cor:witness-polylog}
For each fixed $A>0$,
\begin{equation}\tag{51.17}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 \ll_A N\exp\{-c_A(\log\log N)^2\log\log\log N\},
\end{equation}
unconditionally and without Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.  The cubic supply
gives the additional \textsc{claimed/provisional} estimate
\begin{equation}\tag{51.18}
 \#\{p\leq N:W(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 \ll_A N\exp\{-cA^3(\log\log N)^3\}.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
The $(\log\log N)^3$ exponent shape at polylogarithmic depth has the
near-simultaneous independent antecedent Dahan
\cite[Theorem~4.17]{Dahan} for a different two-parameter $(u,a)$
search-depth statistic without a genus gate; his printed $o(1)$ is
explicitly ineffective.  Equations (51.17)--(51.18) instead bound the
minimal congruence modulus $W$, and Lemmas~\ref{lem:effective-BV}--
\ref{lem:conductor-deletion} give effective constants subject to the
labels displayed here.  This priority distinction is part of the statement,
not an assertion that the statistics are interchangeable.

The formulas are interpreted beyond a fixed large threshold.  The same
bounds hold with cutoff $(\log p)^A$: split at $N^{1/2}$, use comparable
logarithms on the upper half, and absorb the lower half.  Define
\begin{equation}\tag{51.19}
 H_{\rm MOD}(A):\qquad W(p)\leq(\log p)^A
                 \quad\hbox{for every sufficiently large prime }p.
\end{equation}
Theorem~\ref{thm:W-lower} proves that this hypothesis is false for every
$0<A<1$; exactly the exponents $A\geq1$ remain open.  For any $A$ for which
it holds, this unproved pointwise hypothesis implies eventual prime
solvability by Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplier}; the upper tail does not make its
failure set empty.  It is not equivalent to $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$: the former is the Type-II
multiplier statistic (51.1), while the latter is the Type-I factor-in-one-
specified-grade assertion (50.7).  Neither hypothesis implies the other as
stated, although each implies an eventual representation.  Thus (39.35)
and $H_{\rm MOD}$ are respectively a tail and a pointwise question for the
same $W$; $H_{\rm SPF}$ is a different frontier.

\subsection{Effective class supply away from one conductor}
The lower half of the fixed-polylogarithmic supply uses
Bombieri--Vinogradov, not merely the elementary box calculation.  Large
composite progression moduli do not remove a possible Siegel zero, because
their characters may be induced from a small primitive conductor.  The
needed effective form is the following.

\begin{lemma}[effective Bombieri--Vinogradov away from one conductor;
standard cited input with dependency proof]\label{lem:effective-BV}
For every $A>0$ there are effectively computable $B,C,x_0$ such that, for
$x\geq x_0$ and $Q\leq x^{1/2}/(\log x)^B$, there is either no exceptional
conductor or one real primitive conductor $r_x\geq3$ for which
\begin{equation}\tag{51.20}
 \sum_{\substack{q\leq Q\\r_x\nmid q}}
 \max_{(a,q)=1}\left|\psi(x;q,a)-\frac{x}{\varphi(q)}\right|
 \leq\frac{Cx}{(\log x)^A}.
\end{equation}
The same statement holds for dyadic $\pi$-differences by effective partial
summation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Lenstra--Pomerance \cite[Lemma~11.2, pp.~1258--1260]
{LenstraPomerance} give computable absolute constants, one excluded
$s(x)\in[(\log x)^{1/2},e^{\sqrt{\log x}}]$, maxima over
$2\leq y\leq x$ and reduced residues, and error
$Cx^{1/2}Q(\log x)^5+Cxe^{-c\sqrt{\log x}}$ over $s(x)\nmid q$.
Restricting to $Q\leq x^{1/2}(\log x)^{-B}$, and enlarging smaller $Q$ to
$x^{1/3}\log x$, proves (51.20); the maximum in $y$ fixes one conductor
through a dyadic interval.

For completeness, set $R=(\log x)^{B_0}$.  Away from Page, the effective
explicit formula gives $|\psi(y,\chi^*)|\ll ye^{-c\sqrt{\log x}}$ uniformly
on $[x,2x]$, while
$\sum_{q\leq Q,\ r\mid q}1/\varphi(q)\ll\log(2Q)/\varphi(r)$.
At most $\varphi(r)$ primitive characters have conductor $r$, so all
$r\leq R$ cost $O(R\log(2Q))$; the exponential saving absorbs this.
Vaughan's identity and the large sieve handle larger conductors.  Fixed
conductors, including $4$, are checked effectively, and selecting Page at
$2x$ prevents two endpoint deletions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[exceptional-conductor deletion tolerance; proved]
\label{lem:conductor-deletion}
In the boxes used for \eqref{eq:mult-mass}, let $r>1$ be a primitive real
character conductor.  Apart from the explicitly harmless conductor $4$,
there is a prime $p\mid r$ (or $p=2$ when the required extra factor is the
third power of $2$) such that
\begin{equation}\tag{51.21}
                  p\nmid uv\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r\nmid4uv.
\end{equation}
Uniformly in $c,k$, the pairs satisfying (51.21) retain a fixed positive
proportion of the harmonic box mass.  The low-$\omega$ cutoff may be chosen
so that it and (51.21) retain a fixed positive proportion simultaneously.
The same remains true after the low-congestion pruning of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A primitive real conductor is the absolute value of a fundamental
discriminant.  Choose an odd prime factor, or $p=2$ for conductor $8$;
conductor $4$ is handled effectively directly.  Fix $k$.  If $p\mid k$,
the condition $(uv,k)=1$ already excludes $p$.  If $p\nmid k$ and
$p\leq Ck$, the box count with the extra reduced conditions modulo $p$
retains the local proportion
\begin{equation}\tag{51.22}
 \frac{(1-1/p)^2}{1-1/p^2}=\frac{p-1}{p+1}\geq\frac13,
\end{equation}
and $H=K^{10}$ absorbs the boundary for combined modulus at most $Ck^2$.
If $p>Ck$, direct counting of the discarded pairs with $p\mid u$ or
$p\mid v$ costs $O(1/(pk))$ against main term
$\asymp\varphi(k)/k^2$, a ratio $O(k/(p\varphi(k)))=O(1/C)$; endpoint
errors are absorbed as before.  Choose $C$ large.  Increasing the fixed
Rankin cutoff makes the high-$\omega$ tail smaller than any prescribed
fixed fraction, and the high-incidence triples have $o(1)$ of the original
mass by Lemma~\ref{lem:incidence}.  Both deletions therefore leave the same
order of mass.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[effectivity of the repaired chains; proved internally,
record-adjacent and therefore \textsc{claimed/provisional}]
\label{thm:effectivity}
The constants in the source class-mass conclusions (16.8) and
(34.18)--(34.19), in source Theorems 16.4 and 39.7
(paper Theorems~\ref{thm:record} and~\ref{thm:three-quarter}), and in
Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-tail} may all be taken effectively computable.
The record statement retains every correctness and priority qualification
already attached to Theorem~\ref{thm:three-quarter}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
In each dyadic prime block apply Lemma~\ref{lem:effective-BV}; if its
exceptional conductor exists, retain only triples with $r_x\nmid4uv$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:conductor-deletion} preserves the full order of box mass,
uniformly in every subfamily and fibre.  This retained subfamily proves the
original lower class-mass conclusion.  All downstream choices in the
larger sieve, exact CRT moments, Chernoff estimate, Bonferroni polynomial,
integer class counting, and Rankin semigroup transfer are finite and
effective.
\end{proof}
The perimeter is deliberately narrow.  The first sentence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} also contains the source Theorem 34.8 ancillary
$o(1)$ bound for the absolute progression-error sum over \emph{all}
low-congestion triples.  Excluded-conductor deletion effectivizes the class
supply (34.18)--(34.19), not that all-triples $o(1)$ as printed.  The
exceptional-set chain uses the class supply only.  Thus
Theorem~\ref{thm:effectivity} is not an effectivity upgrade of every clause
of Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned}.

\subsection{Pomerance--Weingartner, priority, and blind adjudication}
Pomerance--Weingartner \cite[\S4]{PomeranceWeingartner} prove for their
$k=1$ forced classes
\begin{equation}\tag{51.24}
                  \sum_{\ell\leq X}\frac{f(\ell)}\ell
                  \asymp(\log X)^2.
\end{equation}
Stopping their larger-sieve argument at prime modulus $T$ gives
\begin{equation}\tag{51.25}
 \#\{p\leq N:W_{\rm PW}(p)>T\}
 \ll N\exp\{-c(\log T)^2\},\qquad
 \log N\geq C(\log T)^3,
\end{equation}
where $W_{\rm PW}$ measures that subfamily.  It is also a weaker tail for
$W$.  Their lower class mass explicitly uses Bombieri--Vinogradov after a
polylogarithmic multiplicity bound, their upper mass uses
Brun--Titchmarsh, and their $N$-side is the elementary larger sieve and
Rankin truncation.  Thus PW already contains the square-log truncated tail;
the campaign-derived one-conductor deletion makes the fixed-$m=4$ supply
effective, but PW do not print that repair, and no uniform varying-$m$
effectivity is claimed when the exceptional conductor divides $m$.
The resulting tail exponents are $(\log T)^2$ for the proved PW $k=1$
truncation, $(\log T)^2\log\log T$ for the unconditional fixed-polylog
multipliers with the Section~\ref{sec:cfree} review qualification, and
$(\log T)^3$ for the \textsc{claimed/provisional} power multipliers.
Vaughan's 1970 paper remains inaccessible to this campaign; this is a
comparison with PW's reconstruction, not a direct audit of Vaughan.
Searches found no paper explicitly claiming an effective Vaughan-strength
constant or a Siegel-free proof, but that is a search result, not a
literature guarantee.  PW Theorem 1.3 says only ``There is an absolute
positive constant $C$,'' not that it or the threshold is computable; their
Section 4 says ``We now use the Bombieri--Vinogradov theorem.''  They do not
perform the effectivity repair.
Dahan's statistic and cubic-shape priority are distinguished immediately
after (51.18).

The attested-blind protocol used branch \texttt{blind51}; base
\texttt{4dcf3b7e}\allowbreak\texttt{1e10e31f}\allowbreak\texttt{529f5e28}\allowbreak\texttt{62a2aa23}\allowbreak\texttt{c0820bb6}; freeze
\texttt{7ecd7d51}\allowbreak\texttt{4ae379e9}\allowbreak\texttt{c90d6a7b}\allowbreak\texttt{292d11e8}\allowbreak\texttt{0840b360}; and artifact
\texttt{blind51.md}, SHA-256
\texttt{b0a4a27d}\allowbreak\texttt{ba52fb56}\allowbreak\texttt{3b466742}\allowbreak\texttt{7f2200cc}\allowbreak\texttt{98e670c2}\allowbreak\texttt{7e8fff39}\allowbreak\texttt{0aa846b1}\allowbreak\texttt{039c8b7c}.
Read isolation is self-attested.  Adjudication was
\textsc{converged-with-divergences}: both tails, windows, the
Section~\ref{sec:cfree} replay, conductor repair, (39.35), and PW truncation
converged, but the blind side overclaimed effectivity of the ancillary
all-triples $o(1)$.  The paper's narrower perimeter is correct; no label
changed.

\section{Per-slice vanishing frequency and the empty third layer}
\label{sec:slice-sieve}
The moving genus obstruction is the last fixed-congruence obstruction for
one fixed slice.  On every reduced hard class where the genus permits
positivity, an upper-bound sieve makes residual vanishing relatively sparse.
This is a proved, nonprovisional per-slice almost-all result, not a pointwise
bound or a stack over growing slices.  Here ``hard'' still means prime and
$1\pmod {24}$.

\subsection{The fixed-slice sieve}
Fix positive $c,k$ and put
\begin{equation}\tag{52.1}
 c=st^2,\qquad s=\operatorname {sf}(c),\qquad
 h=4ck,\qquad Q=\lcm(24,h).
\end{equation}
\begin{theorem}[per-slice sieve upper bound; proved]
\label{thm:per-slice-sieve}
Suppose $s\notin\{1,2,3,6\}$.  Fix a reduced class $a\pmod Q$ such that
\begin{equation}\tag{52.2}
 a\equiv1\pmod {24},\qquad (a,h)=1,\qquad \chi_s(a)=-1.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\tag{52.3}
 \#\{p\leq X:p\text{ prime},\ p\equiv a\pmod Q,
                    \ M_{c,k}(p)=0\}
 \ll_{c,k}\frac{X}{(\log X)^{1+2/\varphi(h)}}.
\end{equation}
The finitely many $p$ for which $(c,k)\notin\mathcal B_p$ are included in
the implied constant, which is uniform over the finitely many classes
$a\pmod Q$ satisfying (52.2).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The contrapositive of Theorem~\ref{thm:one-prime-slice}, followed by the
genus calculation for every good $q\nmid Q$, is
\begin{equation}\tag{52.4--52.7}
 \begin{gathered}
 M_{c,k}(p)=0\Longrightarrow
 p^2+4ck^2\text{ has no prime divisor }q\equiv-a\pmod h,\\
 \chi_s(q)=\chi_s(-a)=1,
 \qquad \left(\frac{-4ck^2}{q}\right)
 =\left(\frac{\Delta_s}{q}\right)=1,\\
                         n^2\equiv-4ck^2\pmod q.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The last congruence has exactly two distinct nonzero roots because
$4,k^2,t^2$ are nonzero squares modulo $q$.

Sieve $\mathcal A=\{n\leq X:n\equiv a\pmod Q\}$ by removing zero at every
$\ell\nmid Q$ and the two roots at every good $q$.  For squarefree
$d$ prime to $Q$, CRT and $\rho(\ell)\leq3$ give
\begin{equation}\tag{52.8--52.10}
 \#\mathcal A_d=\frac{X\rho(d)}{Qd}+O(\rho(d)),\qquad
 \sum_{d\leq D}\mu^2(d)\rho(d)\ll D(\log D)^2.
\end{equation}
The exact dimension is $\kappa=1+2/\varphi(h)<2$.  With fixed sufficiently
large $u=u(\kappa)$, $D=X^{1/2}$, and $z=D^{1/u}=X^\eta$, the upper
fundamental lemma gives
\begin{equation}\tag{52.12}
 S(\mathcal A,z)\ll_\kappa\frac XQ V(z)
       +\sum_{d\leq D}\mu^2(d)|r_d|.
\end{equation}
This avoids asserting that the endpoint sieve parameter $2$ works for every
fractional dimension.  The local product is
\begin{equation}\tag{52.13}
 V(z)=\prod_{\substack{\ell\leq z\\\ell\nmid Q}}(1-1/\ell)
 \prod_{\substack{q\leq z\\q\equiv-a\ (h)\\q\nmid Q}}
       \frac{1-3/q}{1-1/q}.
\end{equation}
Mertens in the fixed ray says
$\sum_{q\leq z,\ q\equiv-a\ (h)}q^{-1}
=\varphi(h)^{-1}\log\log z+C(h,-a)+o(1)$, whence
$V(z)\asymp_{c,k,a}(\log z)^{-1-2/\varphi(h)}$.
The elementary remainder is $O(X^{1/2}(\log X)^2)$ and the $O(z)$ small
primes are negligible, proving (52.3).
\end{proof}
The only analytic inputs are the upper-bound fundamental lemma, ordinary
Mertens, and Mertens in one fixed reduced progression.  No
Bombieri--Vinogradov, Siegel--Walfisz, or prime level of distribution is
hidden: primality is majorized by the zero-class sieve.  For explicitly
fixed $(c,k,a)$ these constants are effective in principle.

\begin{corollary}[the congruence-level third layer is empty; proved]
\label{cor:no-third-law}
Under Theorem~\ref{thm:per-slice-sieve}, let $M'$ be any fixed multiple of
$Q$ and let $a'\pmod {M'}$ be a reduced refinement of $a\pmod Q$.  The set
\begin{equation}\tag{52.16}
                  \{p:p\equiv a'\pmod {M'},\ M_{c,k}(p)=0\}
\end{equation}
does not contain all sufficiently large primes in that progression.
Consequently no prime-bearing arithmetic progression inside an unforced
slice-class is an identically vanishing progression.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The fixed-progression prime number theorem gives
\begin{equation}\tag{52.17}
 \#\{p\leq X:p\equiv a'\pmod {M'}\}
 \sim\frac X{\varphi(M')\log X}.
\end{equation}
The vanishing set in the refinement is bounded by (52.3), and the extra
factor $(\log X)^{-2/\varphi(h)}$ tends to zero.
\end{proof}
Thus the universal cores $1,2,3,6$ and the moving genus half in
Theorem~\ref{thm:moving-genus} are the complete congruence-level vanishing
laws for a fixed slice.  The complementary half has no identically
vanishing reduced subprogression.  This does not exclude infinitely many
residual vanishers or a positive slice guaranteed by a fixed divisor.

\begin{proposition}[polylogarithmic uniformity with an epsilon loss; proved,
ineffective]\label{prop:slice-uniform}
Fix $\theta>0$ and $0<\epsilon<1$.  Uniformly for slices and reduced classes
satisfying (52.1)--(52.2) and
\begin{equation}\tag{52.18}
                             h\leq(\log X)^\theta,
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\tag{52.19}
 \#\{p\leq X:p\equiv a\pmod Q,\ M_{c,k}(p)=0\}
 \ll_{\theta,\epsilon}
 \frac X{\varphi(Q)(\log X)^{1+2(1-\epsilon)/\varphi(h)}}.
\end{equation}
The implied constant and threshold are ineffective.  No uniformity for
arbitrary $h=X^{o(1)}$ is asserted.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Retain the two extra root classes only for good primes in
\begin{equation}\tag{52.20}
 y=\exp\{(\log X)^\epsilon\}<q\leq z=X^\eta.
\end{equation}
For $q\geq y$, the hypothesis gives
$h\leq(\log q)^{\theta/\epsilon}$, so Siegel--Walfisz with this fixed
parameter and partial summation yield uniformly
\begin{equation}\tag{52.21}
 \sum_{\substack{y<q\leq z\\q\equiv-a\ (h)}}\frac1q
 =\frac{(1-\epsilon)\log\log X+O(1)}{\varphi(h)}+o(1).
\end{equation}
Ordinary Mertens with primes dividing $Q$ omitted contributes
$\ll Q/(\varphi(Q)\log z)$.  The coarse uniform dimension-three upper
sieve and the elementary remainder $O(X^{1/2}(\log X)^2)$ now give
(52.19).  The usual all-moduli Siegel--Walfisz constants are ineffective,
which is exactly the label in the statement.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finite residual structure and its limits}
The memory-bounded verifier block \texttt{(ay)} factors one norm at a time
and checks all $31$ unforced slices with $ck\leq30$ over the $385$ hard
primes below $30000$; the optional full scan uses all $1181$ below $10^5$.
The residual frequencies broadly track the fixed-slice log-power scale, but
this is finite calibration only.

The $74$ strict gaps $ck_{\rm pr}>ck_{\min}$ in the first range contain
$98$ canonical target divisors at the minimal positive product; the full
range has $221$ events and $308$ divisors.  Individual divisor shapes are
$68,24,6$ below $30000$ and $199,93,16$ below $10^5$ for respectively two
distinct primes, at least three prime factors, and a prime power.  Thus the
majority mechanism is a product of two individually wrong-grade primes,
but products of three or more factors and exponent caps are genuine.  For
example $D=155=5\cdot31$ occurs at $(p,c,k)=(241,11,1)$, $D=75=3\cdot5^2$
at $p=409$ on that slice, and $D=27=3^3$ at
$(p,c,k)=(5953,5,1)$.

After conditioning on both genus signs being negative, define
\begin{equation}\tag{52.26}
 R(A,B)=\frac{\Pr(M_A=0,M_B=0)}{\Pr(M_A=0)\Pr(M_B=0)}.
\end{equation}
Most tested pairs are near one, but stable anticorrelations remain:
$(5,1)$ with $(5,2)$ gives $.529,.577$ and $(5,1)$ with $(10,1)$ gives
$.714,.699$ in the two ranges.  Hence approximate independence is a useful
first calibration for many pairs, not a blanket theorem.  No asymptotic
constant, simultaneous-factorization law, lower bound for residual
vanishing, or pointwise conclusion follows.  In particular, the sieve is
upper-bound only and does not cross the prime-pair parity barrier.

\section{The stacked slice sieve and almost-all $H_{\rm SPF}$}
\label{sec:stacked-sieve}
Distinct slice labels can define the same norm: $(5,2)$ and $(20,1)$ both
have $ck^2=20$.  Their root classes then coincide whenever both ray
conditions accept the prime.  Quotienting duplicate radicands gives the
correct fixed-box exponent.  A canonical squarefree-core subfamily has no
duplicates and proves, for every fixed $A<1$, the effective headline
\begin{equation}\tag{53.1}
 \#\mathcal F_A(X)\ll_A\frac X{\log X}
 \exp\left\{-c_A\frac{(\log\log X)^{3/2}}
                         {\sqrt{\log\log\log X}}\right\},
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal F_A(X)$ is the set of hard primes at which
$H_{\rm SPF}(A)$ fails.
It is an almost-all theorem only.  The square-root loss relative to the
provisional Type-II cubic tail is caused by the Type-I genus-permission
gate.

\subsection{Duplicate-radicand geometry and the fixed stack}
For fixed $T\geq1$, put
\begin{equation}\tag{53.2}
                  Q_T=\lcm(24,\{4ck:ck\leq T\}).
\end{equation}
For integral $T\geq6$, $Q_T=4\lcm(1,\ldots,T)$ and Chebyshev's bounds give
$\log Q_T=\psi(T)+O(1)=O(T)$.  Let $C\pmod {Q_T}$ be reduced and
$C\equiv1\pmod {24}$.  Call $(c,k)$ with $ck\leq T$ active at $C$ when
$\chi_{\operatorname {sf}(c)}(C)=-1$.  The raw sum
\begin{equation}\tag{53.3}
 m_{\rm raw}(C)=
 \sum_{\substack{ck\leq T\\(c,k)\ \text{active at }C}}
                   \frac2{\varphi(4ck)}
\end{equation}
is not the local-density mass.  Put $d=ck^2$ and define
\begin{equation}\tag{53.4}
 \mathcal H_d(C)=\{4ck:ck\leq T,\ ck^2=d,
                              (c,k)\text{ active at }C\}.
\end{equation}
For reduced $b\pmod {Q_T}$ set
\begin{equation}\tag{53.5}
 \begin{split}
 J_C(b)&=\#\{d:\exists h\in\mathcal H_d(C),\ b\equiv-C\pmod h\},\\
 m_\#(C)&=\frac2{\varphi(Q_T)}
       \sum_{b\in(\mathbb Z/Q_T\mathbb Z)^\times}J_C(b).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
A radicand is thereby counted once whenever at least one of its rays accepts
the prime.

\begin{lemma}[root overlap; proved]\label{lem:root-overlap}
Put $d_i=c_i k_i^2$.  If a root class of
\begin{equation}\tag{53.6}
                         x^2\equiv-4d_1\pmod q
\end{equation}
coincides with a root class for $d_2$, then
\begin{equation}\tag{53.7}
                              q\mid4(d_1-d_2).
\end{equation}
Consequently, if $d_1\ne d_2$, all root pairs belonging to slices
$c_i k_i\leq T$ are disjoint at every odd prime $q>T^2$, and no root is
zero there.  If $d_1=d_2$, the root sets coincide identically.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Square a common root and subtract.  At odd $q$ this gives
$q\mid d_1-d_2$.  Since $d_i=(c_i k_i)k_i\leq T^2$, a nonzero difference
has absolute value less than $T^2$.  A zero root similarly forces
$q\mid d_i$; equality of radicands gives identical congruences.
\end{proof}
For $(5,2)$ and $(20,1)$ the moduli are $40$ and $80$.  On an active class
the modulus-$80$ ray lies inside the modulus-$40$ ray, so the corrected
contribution is $2/\varphi(40)=1/8$, not the raw $3/16$.

Let $E_C(X,z)$ count primes $p\leq X$, $p\equiv C\pmod {Q_T}$, for which
there is no prime $q\leq z$ and active $(c,k)$, $ck\leq T$, with
\begin{equation}\tag{53.8}
 q\mid p^2+4ck^2,\qquad q\equiv-C\pmod {4ck}.
\end{equation}
\begin{theorem}[fixed-box stacked sieve; proved, effective]
\label{thm:fixed-stack}
For fixed $T$ there is an effective $\eta_T>0$ such that, uniformly over
the finitely many reduced hard classes $C\pmod {Q_T}$,
\begin{equation}\tag{53.9}
                  E_C(X,X^{\eta_T})
                  \ll_T\frac X{(\log X)^{1+m_\#(C)}}.
\end{equation}
The same estimate holds for primes having no good prime factor of any size
in the box.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take $T_0>\max(Q_T,T^2)$ and sieve
$\mathcal A_C=\{n\leq X:n\equiv C\pmod {Q_T}\}$.  Remove zero at ordinary
primes and accepted root pairs at $q>T_0$.  The genus calculation supplies
two roots, and Lemma~\ref{lem:root-overlap} makes distinct radicands
disjoint, so
\begin{equation}\tag{53.11--53.12}
 \rho_C(q)=1+2J_C(q\bmod Q_T),\qquad
 \#(\mathcal A_C)_d=\frac{X\rho_C(d)}{Q_Td}+O_T(\rho_C(d))
\end{equation}
for squarefree $d$ prime to $Q_T$, with multiplicative $\rho_C$.  Effective
fixed-class Mertens gives
\begin{equation}\tag{53.13--53.14}
 \sum_{T_0<q\leq z}\frac{2J_C(q\bmod Q_T)}q
 =m_\#(C)\log\log z+O_T(1),\qquad
 V_C(z)\asymp_T(\log z)^{-1-m_\#(C)}.
\end{equation}
Apply Friedlander--Iwaniec \cite[Theorem~6.9]{FriedlanderIwaniec} with
$D=X^{1/2}$ and $z=D^{1/u_T}=X^{\eta_T}$ for sufficiently large fixed
$u_T$:
\begin{equation}\tag{53.15}
 S(\mathcal A_C,z)\ll_T\frac X{Q_T}V_C(z)
 +\sum_{d\leq D}\mu^2(d)O_T(\rho_C(d)).
\end{equation}
Since $\rho_C(\ell)=O_T(1)$, the remainder and small primes are negligible.
\end{proof}
For one active slice, $m_\#=2/\varphi(4ck)$, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:per-slice-sieve} is exactly the one-slice specialization.
If a class has no active slice, $m_\#=0$ and only the ordinary
one-dimensional bound remains.  Such escape classes exist for every fixed
$T$: $C=1\pmod {Q_T}$ is one, and Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-core} supplies
infinitely many corresponding primes.

\subsection{Exact genus bits and deterministic permission mass}
The full raw box has the record-only Selberg--Delange asymptotic
\begin{equation}\tag{53.16--53.17}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak M(T)&=\sum_{ck\leq T}\frac2{\varphi(4ck)}
 =\sum_{n\leq T}\frac{2\tau(n)}{\varphi(4n)}
 \sim C_{\rm sl}(\log T)^2,\\
 C_{\rm sl}&=\frac12\prod_{p\ \mathrm{odd}}
 (1-p^{-1})^2\left(1+\frac{p(2p-1)}{(p-1)^3}\right)>0.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Only the effective bound $\mathfrak M(T)\ll(1+\log T)^2$ is used below.  Retain
\begin{equation}\tag{53.19--53.20}
 \mathcal S_T(C)=\{(s,k):s\text{ squarefree},\ sk\leq T,\ \chi_s(C)=-1\},
 \qquad m_0(C)=\sum_{(s,k)\in\mathcal S_T(C)}\frac2{\varphi(4sk)}.
\end{equation}
Their radicands $sk^2$ are distinct.  Quadratic reciprocity gives exactly
\begin{equation}\tag{53.21}
 \chi_s(C)=\prod_{\substack{q\mid s\\q\geq5}}
                         \left(\frac Cq\right).
\end{equation}
As $C$ ranges uniformly over reduced hard classes modulo $Q_T$, the bits
$(C/q)$ for distinct primes $5\leq q\leq T$ are independent uniform signs.
This is a finite Chinese-remainder theorem identity, not a heuristic: the
$-1,2,3$ factors are pinned by $C\equiv1\pmod {24}$, and each odd
prime-power coordinate is independent.

\begin{lemma}[permission-pair mass; proved]\label{lem:permission-mass}
Suppose $5\leq q\leq T/2$ is prime and $\chi_q(C)=-1$.  Then
\begin{equation}\tag{53.22}
                         m_0(C)\gg\frac{\log^2(T/q)}q.
\end{equation}
The constant is absolute.  The same estimate, with another absolute
constant, survives deleting every slice whose modulus $4sk$ is divisible
by one prescribed primitive real-character conductor $r$, provided
$q\nmid r$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Pair each squarefree $u\leq T/q$, $(u,q)=1$, with $qu$.  Equation (53.21)
makes exactly one core active.  For $k\leq T/(qu)$ its weight is at least $1/(2quk)$, and M\"obius
summation gives
\begin{equation}\tag{53.24}
 m_0(C)\gg\frac1q
 \sum_{\substack{u\leq T/q\\u\ \text{ squarefree}\\(u,q)=1}}
 \frac1u\log\frac{T/q}{u}\gg\frac{\log^2(T/q)}q.
\end{equation}
If an exceptional conductor has odd prime factor $\ell$, restrict to
$\ell\nmid uk$; if it is $8$, restrict the relevant cores and $k$ to odd
integers.  These restrictions retain fixed Euler factors and ensure
$r\nmid4sk$.  Conductor $4$ is effectively controlled directly.
\end{proof}
Using only core $q$ would provide one logarithm.  Pairing $u$ with $qu$
provides the second regardless of every later genus bit.

\subsection{The effective growing stack}
Fix $0<A<1$ and put
\begin{equation}\tag{53.25}
 \begin{gathered}
 T=(\log X)^A,\qquad L=\log\log X,\qquad
 K_T=C_0(1+\log^2T),\\
 D=X^{1/3},\qquad z=\exp\left\{\frac{\log D}{BK_T}\right\}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
where $C_0,B$ are sufficiently large absolute constants.  Then
\begin{equation}\tag{53.26}
 \log z\asymp\frac{\log X}{L^2},\qquad
 \log\log z=L+O(\log L),\qquad
 \log\log Q_T=AL+O(1),
\end{equation}
and $Q_T<z$ for every fixed $A<1$ once $X$ is large.

\begin{theorem}[growing stacked slice sieve; proved, effective]
\label{thm:growing-stack}
For every fixed $0<A<1$ there are effectively computable $c_A>0$ and $X_A$
such that the following holds for $X\geq X_A$.  Let $\mathcal E_A(X)$ be
the hard primes $p\leq X$ for which there do not exist an active $(c,k)$
with $ck\leq(\log X)^A$ and a prime $q\leq z$ satisfying
$q\mid p^2+4ck^2$ and $q\equiv-p\pmod {4ck}$.  Then
\begin{equation}\tag{53.27}
 \#\mathcal E_A(X)\ll_A\frac X{\log X}
 \exp\left\{-c_A\frac{L^{3/2}}{\sqrt{\log L}}\right\}.
\end{equation}
The proof uses only the squarefree-$c$ subfamily, so the assertion for all
slices follows a fortiori.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Partition into the $\varphi(Q_T)/\varphi(24)$ reduced hard classes modulo
$Q_T=\exp\{O(T)\}=X^{o(1)}$.  For ray moduli $4sk\leq4T$, effective
Landau--Page-repaired Siegel--Walfisz leaves at most one primitive real
conductor $r=r_X$, $|r|\leq4T$.  If it exists retain only
$r\nmid4sk$; otherwise delete nothing.  Uniformly for retained moduli and
$x\geq Q_T$, the effective explicit formula is
$\psi(x;h,a)=x/\varphi(h)+O(xe^{-c\sqrt{\log x}})$.

Write $m_r(C)$ for the retained mass and start root primes above $Q_T$.
Distinct canonical radicands and partial summation give, uniformly in $C$,
\[
 \sum_{\substack{Q_T<q\leq z\\q\equiv-C\ (4sk)}}q^{-1}
 =\frac{\log\log z-\log\log Q_T}{\varphi(4sk)}
 +o(1/\varphi(4sk)).
\]
The errors over all $O(T\log T)$ labels and the quadratic local terms are
$o(1)$.  Hence ordinary roughness and the added roots give
\begin{equation}\tag{53.30}
 V_C(z)\ll\frac{Q_T}{\varphi(Q_T)\log z}
 e^{-m_r(C)(\log\log z-\log\log Q_T)+o(1)}
 \ll_A\frac{L^{O(1)}}{\log X}e^{-c_A m_r(C)L}.
\end{equation}

The load-bearing uniform-in-dimension input is Friedlander--Iwaniec
\cite[Theorem~6.9]{FriedlanderIwaniec}.  Under
\begin{equation}\tag{53.31}
 \prod_{w\leq\ell<y}(1-g_C(\ell))^{-1}
 \leq K_0\left(\frac{\log y}{\log w}\right)^{\kappa_C},
\end{equation}
if $s=\log D/\log z>9\kappa_C+1$, its main multiplier is
$1+O(e^{9\kappa_C-s}K_0^{10})$ with an absolute constant.  Here the interval
prime estimates give absolute $K_0$, $\kappa_C\ll1+m_r(C)\leq K_T$, and
$s=BK_T$; choose $C_0$ and then $B>9$ with room.  The class remainders,
crudely dominated with $R_T=O(T\log T)$, and the resulting class average are
\begin{equation}\tag{53.32--53.33}
 \begin{gathered}
 Q_T\sum_{d\leq D}\mu^2(d)O(\rho_C(d))\leq X^{1/3+o(1)},\\
 \#\mathcal E(X)\ll_A\frac{XL^{O(1)}}{\log X}
 \frac1{\#\mathcal C_T}\sum_{C\in\mathcal C_T}e^{-c_A m_r(C)L}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
This sieves integers in each class; no prime number theorem modulo growing
$Q_T$ is used.

Set $y=L^{3/2}\sqrt{\log L}$ and ignore the $O(L)$ primes dividing $r$.
Exact genus-bit independence handles delayed permission, while
Lemma~\ref{lem:permission-mass} handles every other class:
\begin{equation}\tag{53.35--53.36}
 \begin{gathered}
 2^{-\pi(y)+O(L)}\leq e^{-cy/\log y}
 \leq e^{-cL^{3/2}/\sqrt{\log L}},\\
 m_r(C)\gg_A L^2/y,
 \qquad e^{-c_A m_r(C)L}\leq e^{-c_A L^3/y}
 =e^{-c_A L^{3/2}/\sqrt{\log L}}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Absorb $L^{O(1)}$ in the exponent.  Every threshold after the one-conductor
deletion is effective.
\end{proof}
The balance is exact: delaying the first negative bit beyond $y$ costs
$\exp\{-cy/\log y\}$, while permission by $y$ costs
$\exp\{-c_A L^3/y\}$.  The endpoint $A=1$ is excluded both because
$Q_T=X^{o(1)}$ is then lost and because the remaining Mertens length
$(1-A)L+O(\log L)$ vanishes.

\begin{corollary}[almost-all $H_{\rm SPF}$; proved, effective]
\label{cor:almost-all-SPF}
For each fixed $0<A<1$, if $\mathcal F_A(X)$ is the set of hard primes
$p\leq X$ at which the event (50.7) fails, then
\begin{equation}\tag{53.37}
 \#\mathcal F_A(X)\ll_A\frac X{\log X}
 \exp\left\{-c_A\frac{(\log\log X)^{3/2}}
                         {\sqrt{\log\log\log X}}\right\}.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
A failure of (50.7) has no good prime of any size.  Fix $A'<A$.  On
$[X/2,X]$, eventually $(\log X)^{A'}\leq(\log p)^A$, so each failure is
counted by Theorem~\ref{thm:growing-stack} with $A'$.  Summation over dyadic
intervals gives (53.37), with constants renamed in terms of $A$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The genus-permission asymmetry}
The honest comparison is: Type I has failure exponent
$(\log\log X)^{3/2}/\sqrt{\log\log\log X}$ and requires independent genus
bits; fixed-polylog Type II has exponent
$(\log\log X)^2\log\log\log X$ and no permission gate; cubic Type II has
exponent $(\log\log X)^3$, no gate, and remains
\textsc{claimed/provisional}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-core}'s escape
classes are exactly the fixed-box
extreme of the Type-I row.  The Type-II stack reaches cubic scale because it
has no permission gate; Type I pays the square root while waiting for the
first negative genus bit.  Dahan \cite[Theorem~4.17]{Dahan} obtains the
cubic shape for his different stacked-shift two-parameter $(u,a)$ depth,
again without a genus gate, and labels its error ineffective.  His statistic
and the Type-I statistic here are not interchangeable; the Landau--Page
deletion makes (53.27) effective.

Corollary~\ref{cor:almost-all-SPF} does not prove the pointwise hypothesis,
bound $D(p)$, remove fixed-box escape progressions, or cross the parity
barrier.

\section{The lower tail and the two-sided frontier}\label{sec:lower-tail}
The upper tails above leave exceptional sets.  Residue-one progressions,
combined with effective Linnik, prove that those sets are genuinely nonempty
infinitely often below logarithmic exponent one.  These are escape theorems,
not nonsolvability theorems.

\subsection{Type II: the minimal multiplier modulus}
For real $T\geq3$, put
\begin{equation}\tag{54.1}
 M(T)=\lcm(1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T\rfloor),
 \qquad \log M(T)=\psi(\lfloor T\rfloor).
\end{equation}
The full least common multiple is needed because (51.1) ranges over every
product $k\ell$, with no primality condition on $\ell$.

\begin{theorem}[Type-II logarithmic lower tail; proved, effective; source
Theorem 54.1]\label{thm:W-lower}
Every prime $p\equiv1\pmod {M(T)}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\tag{54.2}
                              W(p)>T.
\end{equation}
There is an effectively computable absolute constant $C_0$ such that, for
all sufficiently large $T$, one can choose such a prime with
\begin{equation}\tag{54.3}
 p\leq C_0M(T)^{5.2}=\exp\{(5.2+o(1))T\}.
\end{equation}
Consequently these primes are unbounded and
\begin{equation}\tag{54.4}
 \limsup_{p\to\infty\atop p\ \mathrm{prime}}\frac{W(p)}{\log p}
 \geq\frac1{5.2}=0.192307\ldots>0.19.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Suppose data in (51.2) have $m=k\ell\leq T$.  Then $m\equiv3\pmod4$,
$uvw=(m+1)/4$, and $p\equiv1\pmod m$.  If they witnessed $p$, multiplying
$1\equiv-uv^{-1}\pmod m$ by $v$ would give $m\mid u+v$.  But
\[
 2\leq u+v\leq uv+1\leq\frac{m+1}{4}+1<m;
\]
this includes $m=3$, where the upper bound is $2<3$.  Thus (54.2) holds.
The class $1\pmod {M(T)}$ is reduced.  Xylouris's effective Linnik theorem
\cite{Xylouris} gives its least prime at most $C_0M(T)^{5.2}$, and the
effective prime number theorem gives $\psi(T)=T+o(T)$.  Any prime in the
class is at least $M(T)+1$, so primes selected along unbounded $T$ are
unbounded.  Equations (54.2)--(54.3) then give (54.4).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[pointwise Type-II frontier; proved; source Corollary 54.2]
\label{cor:MOD-frontier}
For every $0<A<1$, $H_{\rm MOD}(A)$ is false.  Thus, in its defined
positive-exponent range, exactly $A\geq1$ remains open.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Along Theorem~\ref{thm:W-lower}'s primes,
$W(p)>(1/(5.2+o(1)))\log p>(\log p)^A$.
\end{proof}
If
\[
 E_A(N)=\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W(p)>(\log N)^A\},
\]
then Corollary~\ref{cor:witness-polylog} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:W-lower} give the two-sided summary
\begin{equation}\tag{54.5}
 \begin{array}{ll}
  \#E_A(N)=o(\pi(N))& (A>0),\\
  \#E_A(N)\geq1\text{ for infinitely many }N&(0<A<1).
 \end{array}
\end{equation}
The first line retains the internal-review qualification of (51.17), and
the additional cubic upper bound (51.18) remains
\textsc{claimed/provisional}.  For fixed $T$, the exhibited progression has
relative Dirichlet density
$1/\varphi(M(T))=\exp\{-(1+o(1))T\}$ among the primes.  Thus the lower side
asserts only nonemptiness infinitely often; the true size of $E_A(N)$ is
open.

\subsection{Type I: simultaneous genus forcing}
Put
\begin{equation}\tag{54.6}
 R(T)=\lcm\left(24,
       \prod_{\substack{\ell\leq T\\\ell\ \mathrm{prime}}}\ell\right),
 \qquad \log R(T)=\vartheta(T)+\log4.
\end{equation}
The identity holds for $T\geq3$.

\begin{theorem}[Type-I logarithmic lower tail; proved, effective; source
Theorem 54.3]\label{thm:slice-lower}
For every $T\geq3$ and every prime $p\equiv1\pmod {R(T)}$,
\begin{equation}\tag{54.7}
 \chi_s(p)=1\quad(1\leq s\leq T,\ s\ \mathrm{squarefree}),
 \qquad ck_{\rm pr}(p)>T,\qquad ck_{\min}(p)>T.
\end{equation}
In fact $M_{c,k}(p)=0$ for every admissible slice with $ck\leq T$.
There are unboundedly many such primes satisfying
\begin{equation}\tag{54.8}
 p\leq C_0R(T)^{5.2}=\exp\{(5.2+o(1))T\},\qquad
 \limsup_{p\to\infty\atop p\equiv1\ (24)}
 \frac{ck_{\min}(p)}{\log p}\geq\frac1{5.2}>0.19.
\end{equation}
The convention $ck_{\min}(p)=+\infty$ is allowed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For every odd prime $\ell\leq T$, quadratic reciprocity and
$p\equiv1\pmod4$ give
\begin{equation}\tag{54.9}
 \left(\frac\ell p\right)=\left(\frac p\ell\right)=1.
\end{equation}
The congruence modulo $24$ also gives
$(2/p)=(-1/p)=(3/p)=1$.  The sign and factor $4$ in the fundamental
discriminant $\Delta_s$ therefore have symbol one at $p$; multiplicativity
proves $\chi_s(p)=1$ for every squarefree $s\leq T$.  If $ck\leq T$, then
$s=\operatorname {sf}(c)\leq T$, so (50.3) is empty and
Theorem~\ref{thm:moving-genus} gives $M_{c,k}(p)=0$.

All positive pairs $ck\leq T$ are admissible.  Bertrand's postulate, with
$3\leq T<7$ checked directly, gives $R(T)>4T$, hence $p>4T$; this supplies
the coprimality and size conditions in $\mathcal B_p$.  Apply effective
Linnik to $1\pmod {R(T)}$ and use $\vartheta(T)=T+o(T)$.  Since every prime
in the class is at least $R(T)+1$, the argument for (54.4) proves (54.8).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[pointwise Type-I frontier; proved; source Corollary 54.4]
\label{cor:SPF-frontier}
For every $0<A<1$, $H_{\rm SPF}(A)$ is false; exactly $A\geq1$ remains
open.  More strongly, infinitely many hard primes have no positive slice at
all with
\begin{equation}\tag{54.10}
                  ck\leq(1/(5.2+o(1)))\log p.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
For each $0<A<1$, these primes make the failure set in
Corollary~\ref{cor:almost-all-SPF} nonempty at infinitely many heights,
while (53.37) gives its effective almost-all upper bound.  This quantifies
Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-core}'s genus-forced zeros; it is strictly stronger
than the fixed-divisor non-guarantee in
Theorem~\ref{thm:slice-residue-one}.

The classical effectivity register is exact.  Xylouris
\cite{Xylouris} proves the usable exponent $5.2$, refining Heath--Brown
\cite{HeathBrown}; the implied constant and threshold are effectively
computable, with no ineffective Siegel lower bound.  Rosser--Schoenfeld
\cite{RosserSchoenfeld} give the convenient explicit bounds
$\psi(x)<1.03883x$ and $\vartheta(x)<1.01624x$.  The sharper
$(1+o(1))x$ used above comes from the classical effective prime number
theorem.  No numerical value of $C_0$ is extracted here.

\subsection{The two-sided frontier}
The census puts much of the observed mass at small constant depths, while
(51.17) says that for every fixed $\eps>0$, all but $o(\pi(N))$ primes have
$W(p)\leq(\log p)^\eps$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:W-lower} supplies the effective
opposite extreme.  The resulting proved sandwich is
\begin{equation}\tag{54.11}
 \begin{array}{cl}
 \text{typical:}&W(p)\leq(\log p)^\eps
      \quad\text{for almost all primes, every fixed }\eps>0,\\
 \text{extremal:}&W(p)\geq c\log p
      \quad\text{for infinitely many primes, effectively}.
 \end{array}
\end{equation}
The finite $W$ census, Dahan's different two-parameter depth statistic
\cite{Dahan}, and the different statistic measured in the auro-zera file
suggest logarithmic, logarithm--logarithm, and logarithm-squared
normalizations worth plotting, but the statistics are not interchangeable
and no finite envelope supplies a growth law.  The exact pointwise frontier
is only
\begin{equation}\tag{54.12}
 \boxed{\quad
 \underbrace{A<1}_{\text{proved impossible pointwise}}
 \quad\big|\quad
 \underbrace{A\geq1}_{\text{still open pointwise}}.
 \quad}
\end{equation}
The same proved lower edge holds for $H_{\rm SPF}$.  The extended census and
its deliberately separate finite-search panel appear in
\eqref{eq:remaining-window}; in particular, its symbol $\lesssim2$ is not a
proved upper bound or a conjectured limiting exponent.  Covering every
$m\leq T$ by residue one forces divisibility by $M(T)$, whose logarithm is
$T+o(T)$, so the full-lcm escape route is intrinsically logarithmic.
Theorems~\ref{thm:typeII-certificate}--\ref{thm:typeI-certificate} sharpen
this observation: logarithmic order is optimal inside the
complete-class-plus-generic-least-prime architecture, with a factor-two gap
in the proved Type-II modulus constant and exact saturation in Type I.  They
do not close any part of the open pointwise range in (54.12).  None of these
lower-tail primes is asserted to be an Erd\H{o}s--Straus exception.

\section{The congruence-certificate ceiling and extremal census}
\label{sec:certificate-ceiling}
This section closes the elementary two-sided certificate question left by
Sections~\ref{sec:witness-tail} and~\ref{sec:lower-tail}.  A complete
congruence class certifying escape through depth $T$ must itself have
exponentially large modulus.  The Type-II statement is the prime-progression
extension of the all-integer cylinder wall in
Theorem~\ref{thm:cylinder}, not a new independent supply theorem.  The
Type-I statement uses Corollary~\ref{cor:no-third-law} to turn every
unforced prime core into a required conductor coordinate.  The theorems are
asymptotic and effective.  The censuses below are exact only in their stated
ranges and imply no growth law.

\subsection{Type II: every certificate carries the $3\pmod4$ primes}
Call $a\pmod Q$ a \emph{prime congruence certificate for $W>T$} if
$(a,Q)=1$ and every sufficiently large prime $p\equiv a\pmod Q$ satisfies
$W(p)>T$.  An all-integer certificate has the analogous meaning with every
sufficiently large positive integer in the class; it need not be reduced.
Put
\begin{equation}\tag{56.1}
 P_3(T)=\prod_{\substack{\ell\leq T\\
                 \ell\ \mathrm{prime},\ \ell\equiv3\ (4)}}\ell.
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Type-II congruence-certificate ceiling; proved, effective;
source Theorem 56.1]\label{thm:typeII-certificate}
If $a\pmod Q$ is either a prime or an all-integer congruence certificate for
$W>T$, then
\begin{equation}\tag{56.2}
 P_3(T)\mid Q,\qquad
 \log Q\geq\vartheta(T;4,3)=\left(\frac12+o(1)\right)T.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every fixed $\epsilon>0$, there is an effectively
computable $T_\epsilon$ such that
$Q\geq\exp\{(1/2-\epsilon)T\}$ for $T\geq T_\epsilon$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix a prime $\ell\leq T$, $\ell\equiv3\pmod4$, and put
$A=(\ell+1)/4$.  The divisor $D=1$ in
Lemma~\ref{lem:exact-R} is not merely a formal intrinsic class: its
Lemma~\ref{lem:multiplier} datum is
\begin{equation}\tag{56.3}
 k=1,\quad \ell=\ell,\quad (u,v,w)=(1,A,1),
 \qquad -uv^{-1}\equiv-A^{-1}\equiv-4\pmod\ell.
\end{equation}
Thus every positive integer $n\equiv-4\pmod\ell$ has
$W(n)\leq\ell\leq T$.

Suppose $\ell\nmid Q$.  The Chinese remainder theorem gives infinitely many
positive integers satisfying both $n\equiv a\pmod Q$ and
$n\equiv-4\pmod\ell$, contradicting an all-integer certificate.  In the
prime version their joint class modulo $Q\ell$ is reduced: it is reduced
modulo $Q$ by hypothesis and $-4\not\equiv0\pmod\ell$.  Dirichlet's theorem
therefore gives infinitely many primes in it, again contradicting the
certificate.  Hence every factor in (56.1) divides $Q$.  The final estimate
is the effective prime number theorem in the fixed progression
$3\pmod4$.  This is exactly the mechanism of Theorem~\ref{thm:cylinder},
with the prime-bearing conclusion added.
\end{proof}

The theorem uses only one harvested class at each prime modulus.  It does
\emph{not} prove that the full $\lcm(1,\ldots,T)$ of
Section~\ref{sec:lower-tail} is necessary for an arbitrary certificate.
Composite moduli can meet a progression in only one coset modulo $(Q,M)$,
and the full harvested set need not cover all such cosets.  Thus replacing
the constant $1/2$ in (56.2) by $1$ would require an additional argument.

\subsection{Type I: every prime core is forced into the conductor}
A \emph{hard Type-I congruence certificate through $T$} is a reduced class
$a\pmod Q$ with
\begin{equation}\tag{56.4}
                 24\mid Q,\qquad a\equiv1\pmod {24},
\end{equation}
such that every sufficiently large prime $p\equiv a\pmod Q$ has
$ck_{\min}(p)>T$.  Equivalently, every admissible slice with $ck\leq T$
vanishes on every sufficiently large prime in the class.

\begin{theorem}[Type-I congruence-certificate ceiling; proved, effective;
source Theorem 56.2]\label{thm:typeI-certificate}
Every hard Type-I congruence certificate through $T$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\tag{56.5}
 \prod_{\substack{5\leq\ell\leq T\\\ell\ \mathrm{prime}}}\ell\mid Q,
 \qquad \log Q\geq\vartheta(T)-\log6=T+o(T).
\end{equation}
In particular, for every $\epsilon>0$, effectively for all sufficiently
large $T$, one has $Q\geq\exp\{(1-\epsilon)T\}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $5\leq\ell\leq T$ be prime and suppose $\ell\nmid Q$.  The primitive
quadratic character attached to the core $s=\ell$ is nonconstant modulo its
prime coordinate.  Concretely, choose a nonzero quadratic nonresidue
$b\pmod\ell$, and use the Chinese remainder theorem to refine the
certificate to
\begin{equation}\tag{56.6}
                  p\equiv a\pmod Q,
             \qquad p\equiv b\pmod\ell.
\end{equation}
This is a reduced class modulo $Q\ell$, hence contains infinitely many
primes.  For every such hard prime, quadratic reciprocity and
$p\equiv1\pmod4$ give
\begin{equation}\tag{56.7}
 \chi_\ell(p)=\left(\frac{\Delta_\ell}{p}\right)
       =\left(\frac{-\ell}{p}\right)
       =\left(\frac p\ell\right)=-1.
\end{equation}
The slice $(c,k)=(\ell,1)$ is therefore unforced and is admissible for all
sufficiently large primes in (56.6).  Since $Q\ell$ is a multiple of
$\lcm(24,4\ell)$, Corollary~\ref{cor:no-third-law} says that this reduced
refinement cannot be identically vanishing: arbitrarily far out it contains
a prime with $M_{\ell,1}(p)>0$.  That prime has
$ck_{\min}(p)\leq\ell\leq T$, contradicting the certificate.  Therefore
$\ell\mid Q$ for every prime $5\leq\ell\leq T$.  Taking logarithms and
using the effective prime number theorem proves (56.5).
\end{proof}

The primitivity point in this proof is additive, not multiplicative: the
progression modulo a conductor fills residue classes modulo every conductor
coordinate omitted from $Q$.  Equation (56.6) exhibits both character signs
directly.  No claim that a multiplicative subgroup generated by $Q$
controls the progression is used.

\begin{corollary}[congruence/Linnik recipe; proved within the stated recipe;
source (56.8)]\label{cor:certificate-Linnik}
Suppose a certificate construction at depth $T$ uses
$Q=\exp\{(\alpha+o(1))T\}$, and a least-prime theorem with exponent $L$
selects a certified prime $p\leq Q^{L+o(1)}$.  Here ``certified'' includes
that $p$ lies beyond any finite exceptional prefix allowed by the
certificate definition; a least-prime bound alone does not ensure this for
an arbitrary eventual certificate.  Then
\begin{equation}\tag{56.8}
 W(p)>T\ \hbox{ or }\ ck_{\min}(p)>T,
 \qquad T\geq\frac{1+o(1)}{\alpha L}\log p.
\end{equation}
Theorems~\ref{thm:typeII-certificate} and~\ref{thm:typeI-certificate} force
respectively $\alpha\geq1/2$ and $\alpha\geq1$.  Thus the coefficient
available from this generic recipe is at most $2/L$ in Type II and at most
$1/L$ in Type I.  The residue-one choices of
Theorems~\ref{thm:W-lower} and~\ref{thm:slice-lower} have $\alpha=1$ in
both frames.  They therefore saturate the Type-I certificate rate and are
within a factor two of the proved Type-II ceiling; in both frames they
establish the optimal logarithmic order for a
certificate-plus-least-prime argument, up to the least-prime exponent.
With the conservative effective Linnik value $L=5.2$, their coefficient is
$1/5.2$.
\end{corollary}

% wave-21 review repair: keep the GRH conclusion conditional and separate
% from the unconditional effective certificate ceilings.
\begin{remark}[GRH least-prime consequence; conditional; source (56.9)]
\label{rem:GRH-certificate}
Conditional on the unproved Generalized Riemann Hypothesis, the standard
least-prime bound permits $L=2+\epsilon$.  The same residue-one
constructions therefore give, respectively along unbounded prime sequences,
\begin{equation}\tag{56.9}
 W(p)> (1/2-\epsilon)\log p,
 \qquad ck_{\min}(p)> (1/2-\epsilon)\log p,
\end{equation}
after harmlessly changing $\epsilon$.  This statement is conditional;
(56.2) and (56.5) are unconditional and effective.
\end{remark}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Assessment 56.2a (the congruence wall).}
The proved conclusion is that a \emph{uniform complete-class} certificate
through $T$ has exponential modulus.  Consequently, inside the
Section~\ref{sec:lower-tail} architecture---complete congruence certificate,
modulus $\exp\{\Theta(T)\}$, then a generic least-prime theorem---a
superlogarithmic lower bound would have to abandon at least one ingredient.
The natural alternative is a noncongruence certificate on a sifted set of
primes, in the critical-window territory of Section~\ref{sec:window}.  The
ceiling does not logically prohibit every congruence-informed construction:
it says nothing about an accidentally tiny first prime in a chosen class,
or a modulus much larger than $\exp\{\Theta(T)\}$.  Accordingly this is an
architecture wall, not a theorem that every possible extremal proof beyond
$C\log p$ must be noncongruential.

\subsection{The exact $W$ census}
\medskip\noindent\textbf{Computational 56.3 (exact stated ranges;
informational).}
Verifier block \texttt{(bc)} harvests the complete intrinsic class set of
Lemmas~\ref{lem:multiplier} and~\ref{lem:exact-R},
$\{-4D\pmod M:D\mid((M+1)/4)^2\}$, for every $M\leq3000$,
$M\equiv3\pmod4$.  It then scans every hard prime in increasing order and
stops at the first harvested modulus.  All $9{,}732$ hard primes below
$10^6$ resolve within the cap.  Their strict records are
\begin{equation}\tag{56.10}
\begin{gathered}
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrrr}
p&73&193&1201&2521&3361&33289&90841\\
W(p)&7&15&31&47&99&155&167
\end{array}\\[3pt]
\begin{array}{c|rrrrrr}
p&144169&167521&225289&361321&915961&954409\\
W(p)&191&259&279&287&303&335
\end{array}
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
With \texttt{ES\_FULL\_SCAN=1}, the streamed, chunked scan covers all
$82{,}887$ hard primes below $10^7$.  The only later records are
\begin{equation}\tag{56.11}
 (p,W(p))=(1853329,383),\qquad(2031121,2495),
\end{equation}
and the running maximum remains $2495$ through the endpoint.  These values
are exact minima in the harvested family because every smaller eligible
modulus was tested; the cap is not hit.

The requested normalized maxima, using natural logarithms, are
\textsc{info-only}:
\begingroup
\scriptsize
\setlength{\arraycolsep}{3pt}
\begin{equation}\tag{56.12}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 p\text{-range}&\max W/\log p&\max W/(\log p\log\log p)&
                    \max W/(\log p)^2\\ \hline
p<10^6&24.330286\ (954409,335)&9.277839\ (954409,335)&
       1.836625\ (225289,279)\\
p<10^7&171.783468\ (2031121,2495)&64.198698\ (2031121,2495)&
       11.827479\ (2031121,2495)
\end{array}
\end{equation}
\endgroup
Each parenthesis is $(p,W(p))$.  At $p=10^7$, the unconditional
Section~\ref{sec:lower-tail} coefficient gives
$(1/5.2)\log p=3.0996\ldots$.  This comparison must not mix registers:
Section~\ref{sec:lower-tail} guarantees rare residue-one escapes at a height
bounded by $\exp\{(5.2+o(1))T\}$, whereas (56.10)--(56.12) record whatever
finite primes happen to be extremal below a moderate endpoint.  For example,
the generic height attached to $T=2495$ is astronomically beyond this scan.
The census neither samples that constructed sequence nor estimates a tail
density, and the large isolated jump in (56.11) supports no growth law.

\subsection{The Type-I extremal extension}
\medskip\noindent\textbf{Computational 56.4 (exact stated ranges;
informational).}
The same block factors one norm at a time, skips the exact genus-forced signs
before factorization, and streams unresolved primes in product order.  For

\begin{equation}\tag{56.13}

With \texttt{ES\_FULL\_SCAN=1}, all $9{,}732$ hard primes below $10^6$ are

\begin{equation}\tag{56.15}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\text{statistic and range}&\max X/\log p&
 \max X/(\log p\log\log p)&\max X/(\log p)^2\\ \hline
ck_{\rm pr},\ p<3\cdot10^5&38.250190\ (171481,461)&
 15.366153\ (171481,461)&3.173703\ (171481,461)\\

Here $X$ in the header denotes the statistic in the first column, and each
