once.  An explicit leftover assertion fails if a prime requires $M>30000$.
It independently obtains all $244216$ classes, all $719781$ primes and
minima, and largest needed modulus $2495$.

The transient staged $p<10^7$ run reported all $82887$ primes and the old
record tables; its claimed second implementation on $1000$ seeded primes
was also not preserved.  Block \texttt{(bl)} durably rebuilds both class
systems and, between default and optional modes, replays the displayed
records, four late rows, and $1000$ seeded hard primes below $10^5$.
The independent review script instead recomputes the complete $10^8$ census
and every table below from scratch.

of a parallel argument.

\subsection{Replay scope, review pedigree, and honest walls}
\medskip\noindent\textbf{Computational 65.3 (bounded replay).}
By default block \texttt{(bl)} freshly builds both Python-integer class
systems through $M=3000$, asserts rowwise equality, replays every record
through $10^6$ and all four late rows including exact fraction sums, compares
$1000$ seeded hard-prime minima below $10^5$, pins the small-prefix parity
count $(706,475)$, and checks the $p+4$ prediction on records through
$10^6$.  With \texttt{ES\_FULL\_SCAN=1}, it uses $M\leq30000$ and replays
all $15$ record primes below $10^8$, certifying absence of smaller moduli for
those primes only.  It does \emph{not} rerun the $719781$-prime census; that
is the separate committed independent script.

The original author reported $1.37$ seconds for the $10^7$ process and
$2.65$ seconds with $120960$ KiB maximum resident memory for $10^8$; because
the runner was not preserved, these are historical, not reproducible from
those commits.  Block \texttt{(bl)} was reported at $0.029$ seconds by
default and $0.663$ seconds in isolation with the full path.  Scan speed
reflects tiny typical minima and replaces none of the exact checks.

The wave-27 maximum-severity review returned
\textsc{sound-after-repairs}.  Its durable from-scratch script enumerated
exactly $719781$ hard primes in the exclusive range
$73\leq p<100000000$, ending at $99999721$, produced all $7500$ rows and
$244216$ classes, resolved every prime with last necessary row $M=2495$,
and reproduced all $15$ records.  It matched every one of the $21$ block
counts and both normalized columns to six decimals, the global suprema
$171.783468/64.198698$, selected parity $49975/82887$, intrinsic split
$(49975,32454,458)$, and zero $p+4$-prediction violations.  The review also
repaired the selected-$D$ convention and narrowed claims about the missing
transient source without changing a table entry.  The independent census
passed in $1.39$ seconds with $108424$ KiB peak resident memory; bounded
block \texttt{(bl)} remained deterministic and did not claim to rerun it.
These are internal computational checks, not external validation.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Honest walls.}
This census sharpens only the finite-search panel behind the informal
$1\leq A\lesssim2$ window.  No new record after $10^7$, parity stability,
and normalized block values prove nothing about $H_{\rm MOD}(A)$: they give
no upper bound for any $A\geq1$ and do not alter the proved refutation for
$A<1$.  No growth law, asymptotic fit, independence assertion, or tail-density
claim follows from (65.2)--(65.4).  Every conclusion is
Computational/INFO finite-range evidence; $H_{\rm MOD}$ and the
Erd\H{o}s--Straus conjecture are untouched.

\section{General-numerator tails and the effectivity perimeter}
\label{sec:general-m-tails}
This section truncates the general-numerator machinery; it does not extend
its range.  The fixed-polylogarithmic row has the internal review
qualification of Lemma~\ref{lem:truncated-cfree} and Section~\ref{sec:cfree},
but uses neither Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned} nor
Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned}.  The cubic row and record recovery inherit them
and remain \textsc{claimed/provisional}.  Effectivity below means
computability of constants conditional on those correctness labels.  Put
\begin{equation}\tag{55.1}
 \lambda_m=\frac{\eta_1(m)}{\varphi(m)},\qquad
 \theta_m=\frac{\eta_2(m)}{\varphi(m)};
\end{equation}
both are $\asymp1/m$ up to absolute constants.

\subsection{The minimal general-numerator witness modulus}
For $m\geq3$ and positive $p$, define
\begin{equation}\tag{55.2}
 W_m(p)=\min k\ell,
\end{equation}
where the minimum is over positive $k,\ell,u,v,w$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\tag{55.3}
 k\ell\equiv-1\pmod m,\qquad \frac{k\ell+1}{m}=uvw,
 \qquad pv\equiv-u\pmod {k\ell};
\end{equation}
put $W_m(p)=+\infty$ if no datum exists.  The coordinate $\ell$ need not be
prime.  Lemma~\ref{lem:m-identity} turns $W_m(p)\leq T$ into a representation
of $m/p$, and at $m=4$ this is exactly (51.1)--(51.2).

Let $t=\log X$, $KX\leq T$, and use the canonical boxes from the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned}.  Their two masses are
\begin{equation}\tag{55.4}
 M_1=\lambda_mt^2\log K,
 \qquad M_2=\theta_mt^3\quad(K=X^\kappa).
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[$m$-uniform truncated assembly; proved internally with the
Section~\ref{sec:cfree} review qualification; source Lemma 55.1]
\label{lem:m-truncated}
Fix $B>0$.  Uniformly for $m\leq t^B$, if $K$ is a sufficiently large fixed
power of $t$, $KX\leq T$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{55.6}
                     \log N\geq C_Bt(1+M_1),
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}\tag{55.5}
 \#\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W_m(p)>T\}
       \ll_B N\exp\{-c_BM_1\}.
\end{equation}
For fixed $0<\kappa<1/240$, if $K=X^\kappa$, $KX\leq T$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{55.8}
                     \log N\geq C_{B,\kappa}t(1+M_2),
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}\tag{55.7}
 \#\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W_m(p)>T\}
       \ll_{B,\kappa}N\exp\{-c_{B,\kappa}M_2\}.
\end{equation}
The second assertion is \textsc{claimed/provisional} through
Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned}.  Every constant is effective in the precise
sense of Subsection~\ref{sec:m-effectivity}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The full coprime multiplier family has mass comparable to $M_1$; replacing
$\eta_1$ by $\eta_2$ in the upper profile and moments changes only an
absolute factor.  The cubic family has mass $M_2$ by (43.20), (43.27), and
the relative-mass argument
(43.36)--(43.38).  Choose the small-prime quarantine and even Bonferroni
degree of order $M_i$ when it grows, and fixed degree otherwise.  The exact
ledger is
\begin{equation}\tag{55.9}
 \deg=O(1+M_i),\qquad
 \log d_{\rm term},\ \log\sum|c_{\rm term}|=O_B\{t(1+M_i)\}.
\end{equation}
Conditions (55.6) and (55.8) pay this ledger.  Every retained atom has
modulus at most $KX\leq T$; primes dividing $m$ and fixed initial ranges are
absorbed effectively.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[general-$m$ master tails; status as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:m-truncated}; source Theorem 55.2]
\label{thm:general-m-tails}
Fix $B>0$.  Uniformly for $m\leq(\log T)^B$, put
\begin{equation}\tag{55.10}
 \mathcal M_1(m,T)=\lambda_m(\log T)^2\log(2+\log T),\qquad
 \mathcal M_2(m,T)=\theta_m(\log T)^3.
\end{equation}
Set $\mathcal P_{m,T}(N)=
\#\{p\leq N:p\text{ prime},\ W_m(p)>T\}$.  With effective constants
depending only on the displayed fixed parameters,
\begin{equation}\tag{55.11}
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal P_{m,T}(N)&\ll_BNe^{-c_B\mathcal M_1(m,T)},&
 \log N&\geq C_B\{\log T+(\log T)\mathcal M_1(m,T)\},\\
 \mathcal P_{m,T}(N)&\ll_{B,\kappa}Ne^{-c_{B,\kappa}\mathcal M_2(m,T)},&
 \log N&\geq C_{B,\kappa}\{\log T+(\log T)\mathcal M_2(m,T)\}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The first line uses only the Layer-1 supply.  The second is
\textsc{claimed/provisional}.  No uniformity is asserted when $m$ exceeds
every fixed power of $\log T$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Take $X=T^{1/2}$.  In the first row choose $K=t^{D(B)}$ large enough for the
sign selection below; then $KX\leq T$ and
$\log K\asymp_B\log(2+t)$.  In the second, rescale $X$ by a harmless fixed
power so $K=X^\kappa$ and $KX\leq T$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:m-truncated} gives
both rows; bounded $T$ is absorbed in the constants.
\end{proof}
At the top of the cubic window,
$\log N\asymp\theta_m(\log T)^4$, so the saving is
$\theta_m^{1/4}(\log N)^{3/4}$, exactly (43.35).  In the fixed-gap range
$m\leq(\log N)^{3-\eps}$ the extra $\log T$ term is smaller and the
$m\leq t^4$ ledger closes.  The first row similarly recovers the scale
$\lambda_m^{1/3}(\log N)^{2/3}(\log\log N)^{1/3}$ and the fixed-gap range
$m\leq(\log N)^{2-\eps}$.  These are consistency checks, not range or status
upgrades.

\begin{corollary}[general-$m$ polylogarithmic tails; source Corollary 55.3]
Fix $A,B>0$ and $m\leq(\log\log N)^B$.  With $p$ restricted to primes,
\begin{equation}\tag{55.12}
 \begin{split}
 \#\{p\leq N:W_m(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 &\ll_{A,B}N\exp\{-c_{A,B}\lambda_m
       (\log\log N)^2\log\log\log N\},\\
 \#\{p\leq N:W_m(p)>(\log N)^A\}
 &\ll_{A,B}N\exp\{-c_{A,B}\theta_m(\log\log N)^3\}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The second line is \textsc{claimed/provisional}.  The almost-all result does
not prove the pointwise hypothesis
\begin{equation}\tag{55.13}
 H_{{\rm MOD},m}(A):\quad W_m(p)\leq(\log p)^A
 \quad\hbox{for every sufficiently large prime }p.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\subsection{The conductor coupled to $m$}\label{sec:m-effectivity}
The prime progression in the general-$m$ supply is
\begin{equation}\tag{55.14}
                  q=muv,\qquad \ell\equiv-k^{-1}\pmod q,
\end{equation}
not modulo $uv$ or $\lcm(m,uv)$.  Thus if a Page conductor $r$ divides
$m$, every modulus is divisible by $r$ and deletion would erase the entire
supply.  Retaining the exceptional term gives the effective form
\begin{equation}\tag{55.15}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal E_r(y;q,a)&=\psi(y;q,a)-\frac y{\varphi(q)}
 +\frac{\1_{r\mid q}\chi_r(a)y^\beta}{\beta\varphi(q)},\\
 \sum_{q\leq Q}\max_{(a,q)=1}\max_{x\leq y\leq2x}
 |\mathcal E_r(y;q,a)|&\ll_A\frac x{(\log x)^A}.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Here $Q\leq x^{1/2}/(\log x)^{B_A}$; constants and thresholds are
effective, and the exceptional term is absent if there is no Page
character.  This is the displayed-exceptional-character variant obtained by
retaining the one primitive real character in the proof of
Lenstra--Pomerance \cite[Lemma~11.2]{LenstraPomerance}; their lemma itself
prints the excluded-conductor form.

\begin{lemma}[$m$-uniform Page repair; proved; source Lemma 55.4]
\label{lem:m-Page}
Uniformly for $m\leq t^B$, effective aggregate lower class mass holds for
exactly the full coprime multiplier family used by the Layer-1 chain and the
structured fibres $\mathcal J_c$ used by the $c$-free chain.  This is not a
per-multiplier lower profile and not an arbitrary-sparse-subfamily claim.
The conductor cases are:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,label=\arabic*.]
\item With no exceptional conductor, retain every box.
\item If $r\nmid m$, choose a prime $a$ with
$v_a(r)>v_a(m)$ and impose $a\nmid uv$.  Then $r\nmid muv$, including when
$r$ and $m$ share other prime-power factors.
\item If $r\mid m$, retain exactly the multipliers satisfying
\begin{equation}\tag{55.16}
                         \chi_r(-k^{-1})=-1.
\end{equation}
For every progression actually retained, the exceptional term in (55.15)
then adds to the ordinary main term.  This family retains a fixed positive
fraction of the aggregate mass needed downstream.
\end{enumerate}
The low-$\omega$ deletion and low-congestion pruning may be imposed
simultaneously.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
In case 2, omitting $a$ from $uv$ leaves the $a$-adic exponent of $muv$
below that of $r$; Lemma~\ref{lem:conductor-deletion}'s local proportion
and large-$a$ estimate apply pointwise in $k$.

Suppose $r\mid m$.  The lifted nonprincipal real character takes each sign
on half the units modulo $m$.  Uniformly in reduced $b\pmod m$, M\"obius
expansion gives
\begin{equation}\tag{55.17}
 \sum_{Y<k\leq2Y\atop k\equiv b\ (m)}\frac{\varphi(k)}k
 =\frac Ym\prod_{p\nmid m}(1-p^{-2})+O(\log(2Y)).
\end{equation}
After summing favorable classes and dividing by $k\asymp Y$, intervals with
$Y\geq Cm^2$ contribute $\gg\eta_1(m)$.  If $F_r$ is the set in (55.16),
$H_m(K)=\sum_{k\leq K,(k,m)=1}\varphi(k)/k^2$, and $K\geq m^D$ for a fixed
large $D$, then effectively
\begin{equation}\tag{55.17a}
 h(F_r)\geq c_0\eta_1(m)\log K\geq c_1H_m(K).
\end{equation}
The cubic family has $K=e^{\kappa t}\gg m^D$; the Layer-1 tail uses
$K=t^{D(B)}$.

For the structured fibre
$\mathcal J_c=\{k:(k,m)=(k,c)=1\}$, (43.36) gives the adversarially safe
subtraction
\begin{equation}\tag{55.17b}
 h(F_r\cap\mathcal J_c)
 \geq h(F_r)-H_m(K)
  \sum_{p\mid c,\ p\mid L_{m,K}}\frac1p
 \geq(c_1-Z(c))H_m(K).
\end{equation}
Thus the same Chernoff event as (43.37), now with $Z(c)\leq c_1/2$, leaves
a fixed positive favorable mass without asserting character balance in an
arbitrary sparse family.  If an incidence statement formally requires
$1\in\mathcal J$, adjoin it only for bookkeeping and omit its triples from
the BV lower sum when its sign is unfavorable.  Increasing the low-$\omega$
cutoff and applying the existing low-congestion pruning preserve the order
of mass.
\end{proof}

This quantifier boundary is load-bearing: an arbitrary sparse
$\mathcal J$ could contain only the unfavorable sign.  The full Layer-1
family and the fibres $\mathcal J_c$ are exactly the two downstream families
for which Lemma~\ref{lem:m-Page} proves enough positive mass.

\begin{theorem}[effectivity perimeter; proved internally,
status-preserving; source Theorem 55.5]\label{thm:m-effectivity}
``Effective'' means that constants and thresholds are computable uniformly
in the theorem's displayed fixed-gap range.  The exact perimeter is
\begin{equation}\tag{55.18}
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{statement}&\text{effective?}&\text{inheritance}\\ \hline
 \text{Layer-1 tails}&\text{yes}
   &\substack{\text{Section~39 review}\\\text{no 34.8 or 43.7}}\\
 \text{thinned-cubic tails}&\substack{\text{yes, conditional}\\
                                      \text{on correctness}}
   &\substack{\text{\textsc{claimed/provisional}}\\\text{34.8 and 43.7}}\\
 \text{Theorem~43.12}&\substack{\text{yes, conditional}\\
                                \text{on correctness}}
   &\substack{\text{\textsc{claimed/provisional}}\\
              \text{34.8, 43.7, and Section~39.7}}
\end{array}
\end{equation}
In every row, $r\nmid m$ uses deletion in $uv$, while $r\mid m$ uses the
favorable $k$-half.  The cubic rows effectivize only the surviving class
mass, followed in Theorem~43.12 by the existing effective ledger and
semigroup transfer.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:m-Page} repairs the only Siegel-sensitive lower
prime-progression input.  Shiu, Brun--Titchmarsh, the box and incidence
bounds, exact CRT moments, Chernoff, Bonferroni, integer class counting, and
the Rankin semigroup transfer are effective.  At the cubic top, (55.8) is
the existing (43.40) ledger, so no parameter or range changes.
\end{proof}
The table does not effectivize the inherited ancillary absolute-error
assertion in Theorem~\ref{thm:m-pruned} over \emph{all} low-congestion
triples.  Restoring deleted unfavorable triples restores the unresolved
exceptional term; no row of (55.18) uses that assertion.

\subsection{Comparison and finite companion}
Truncating Pomerance--Weingartner's general-$m$, $k=1$ supply gives mass
$\asymp(\log T)^2/\varphi(m)$.  The ratios of the two masses in (55.10) to
that benchmark are
\begin{equation}\tag{55.19}
              \eta_1(m)\log\log T,
              \qquad \eta_2(m)\log T.
\end{equation}
Thus PW's pure $\varphi(m)^{-1}$ dependence is formally stronger below the
crossovers where either ratio is one, and the multiplier exponents are
formally stronger above.  For every fixed $B$, the below-crossover regions
eventually lie outside the proved window $m\leq(\log T)^B$; this separates
the unrestricted formal comparison from actual uniformity.  The cubic side
is provisional, PW is proved, and unknown constants preclude a finite
crossover.  At the optimized $N$-window the standing comparison remains
(43.33)--(43.34), including PW's $\varphi(m)^{1/3}$ uniformity.  No
pointwise theorem or range extension follows.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Computational 55.6 (exact finite regression;
informational).}
Verifier block \texttt{(bb)} harvests all classes (55.3) for
$m\in\{3,5,6,7\}$ and $k\ell\leq1500$.  For primes $p\leq10^5$ prime to
$m$, the counts with $W_m(p)>25,100,400$ and those still unharvested are
\begin{equation}\tag{55.20}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 m&\#p&\text{three tail counts}&\text{unharvested at }1500\\ \hline
 3&9591&(0,0,0)&0\\
 5&9591&(875,61,5)&1\\
 6&9590&(3136,663,77)&17\\
 7&9591&(2222,438,35)&10
\end{array}
\end{equation}
The block independently checks the intrinsic class description, reproduces
the $m=4$ classes from \texttt{(ax)}, and verifies
\begin{equation}\tag{55.21}
 \eta_2(3)=\frac9{11},\quad \eta_2(5)=\frac{25}{29},\quad
 \eta_2(6)=\frac{36}{55},\quad \eta_2(7)=\frac{49}{55}.
\end{equation}
For the Page toys $(m,r)=(5,5),(6,3)$, both signs occur and the favorable
side retains between one third and three quarters of the finite harmonic
weight.  This is a finite shadow of (55.17a), not evidence for its
asymptotic uniform assertion.
\endgroup

\section{Residue-dispersion frontier}\label{sec:dispersion-frontier}
Put
\[
 L=\log X,\qquad z=\frac{L^3\log\log L}{\log L},\qquad
 \Lambda=\frac{L^3}{\log L}.
\]
For a composite $z$-rough $M\leq X$, $M\equiv3\pmod4$, choose the least
positive divisor $D\mid((M+1)/4)^2$ in each fibre of
$D\mapsto-4D\pmod M$, and delete a representative when its class is implied
by a prime-modulus atom.  Call the remaining set $\mathcal D^\circ(M)$.
With the exact conditional weight
\[
 \kappa(M)=\prod_{\substack{q\mid M,\ z<q\leq Y\\q\equiv3(4)}}
                \frac q{q-F(q)},
 \qquad
 n_{M,p}(a)=\#\{D\in\mathcal D^\circ(M):-4D\equiv a\pmod p\},
\]
the pair-level dispersion quantity is exactly
\begin{equation}\tag{40.5}
 \mathcal V_X=\sum_{z<p\leq X}p\sum_{a\bmod p}
 \left(\sum_{\substack{M\leq X,
      \ M\ \text{composite},\ P^-(M)>z\\
      M\equiv3(4),\ p\mid M}}
      \frac{\kappa(M)}M n_{M,p}(a)\right)^2.
\end{equation}
This is the residue-dispersion reformulation; canonical deduplication,
prime powers, and the prime-implied deletion are part of the formula.

Write $D=R^2/s$, with $s\mid\rad(R)$ and $M=4Rk-1$.  For
$p\nmid4R$, let $c_p(R)\in\{1,\ldots,p-1\}$ be the inverse of $4R$ modulo
$p$.  The condition $p\mid M$ gives $k=c_p(R)+jp$.  Splitting into the
endpoint $j=0$ and regular tail $j\geq1$ gives vectors
$u^{\rm end}_{p,a}$ and $u^{\rm reg}_{p,a}$.  The regular-tail theorem is
proved, with the full canonical and retention restrictions allowed:
\begin{equation}\tag{40.14}
 \mathcal V_X^{\rm reg}
 :=\sum_{z<p\leq X}p\sum_a(u^{\rm reg}_{p,a})^2
 \ll L^2\log L+\frac{L^7}{z\log z}=o(\Lambda^2).
\end{equation}
Indeed the contribution of fixed $D$ is at most $CL/(pR_0(D))$; after
expansion, $p\mid D-E$, while
$\sum_D L/R_0(D)\ll L^3$ and
$\sum_D(L/R_0(D))^2\ll L^2$.  For $D\ne E$ there are at most
$2L/\log z$ prime divisors above $z$, and for $D=E$ Mertens' estimate costs
$O(\log L)$, giving (40.14).

The exact remaining pair target is therefore
\begin{equation}\tag{40.18}
 \boxed{\quad \mathcal V_X^{\rm end}
 =\sum_{z<p\leq X}p\sum_a(u^{\rm end}_{p,a})^2
 =O(\Lambda^2).\quad}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, in divisor variables, it is the explicit short-cofactor
estimate
\begin{equation}\tag{40.19}
\begin{aligned}
 \sum_{z<p\leq X}p\sum_{a\bmod p}
 \Bigg(&\sum_{\substack{R,s,c,q:\ s\mid\rad(R),\ 1\leq c<p\\
       pq=4Rc-1\leq X,\ P^-(pq)>z,\ pq\equiv3(4)\\
       R^2/s\equiv-4^{-1}a\ (p),\ q>1\ ({\rm so}\ pq\ \text{composite})\\
       R^2/s\ \text{is\ the\ retained\ representative\ at}\ pq}}
       \frac{\kappa(pq)}{pq}\Bigg)^2
 \ll\Lambda^2.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Every term has $q>z$ and $q<4R$.

\subsection{Endpoint sparsity and proved ranges}
For $(p,4R)=1$, let $q_p(R)$ be the unique integer in $[1,4R)$ with
\begin{equation}\tag{42.1}
                         pq_p(R)\equiv-1\pmod{4R}.
\end{equation}
Let $I_p(R,s)$ assert all the conditions in (40.19), including
$s\mid\rad(R)$, $q_p(R)>z$, $pq_p(R)\leq X$, roughness, and canonical
retention.  Then endpoint incidences are exactly the pairs with
$I_p(R,s)=1$, and
\begin{equation}\tag{42.2}
 q=q_p(R),\quad z<q<4R,\quad 1\leq c=\frac{pq+1}{4R}<p,
 \quad \frac z4<R\leq\frac{X+1}4.
\end{equation}
Thus there is one cofactor for each $(p,R)$, not one for each $p$; the
moving multiplicity $2^{\omega(R)}$ in $s$ remains.

Put $A=(pq+1)/4=Rc$.  The complementary divisor to $D=R^2/s$ is
$A^2/D=c^2s$, and hence
\begin{equation}\tag{42.3}
 D\equiv\overline{16c^2s}\pmod p,
 \qquad -4D\equiv-\overline{4c^2s}\pmod p.
\end{equation}
Two endpoint incidences over $p$ collide exactly when
$c^2s\equiv c'^2s'\pmod p$.  Distinct incidences cannot have equal integer
shifts: $R^2/s=R'^2/s'$ forces the common value's radical root
$R=R_0(D)=R'$, then $s=s'$, and (42.1) gives the same $q$.  The endpoint
energy therefore has only genuine nonzero divisibility coincidences off the
diagonal.

\begin{theorem}[fixed endpoint fibres]\label{thm:fixed-fibre}
For every fixed squarefree $s$, uniformly in $s$,
\begin{equation}\tag{42.4}
 \sum_{z<p\leq X}p\sum_{a\bmod p}
       (u^{\rm end,(s)}_{p,a})^2
 \ll L^3+L^2\log L=o(\Lambda^2).
\end{equation}
A prescribed set of $K_0$ squarefree fibres has energy $O(K_0^2L^3)$ and
therefore closes when $K_0=O(L^{3/2}/\log L)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Drop canonical restrictions by positivity and use $\kappa\leq2$.  For
$1\leq c<p$, put
\[
 B_{p,c}^{(s)}=\sum_{\substack{z<q\leq X/p\\pq\equiv-1\ (4c)\\
                  s\mid\rad((pq+1)/(4c))}}\frac1q.
\]
If $p\nmid s$, the map $c\mapsto-\overline{4c^2s}$ has fibres of size at
most two, and therefore
\[
 p\sum_a(u^{\rm end,(s)}_{p,a})^2
 \leq\frac8p\sum_{c<p}(B_{p,c}^{(s)})^2.
\]
The $q$'s lie in one progression of spacing $4c$.  Separating
its first member and writing $\ell_p=1+\log^+(X/(pz))$ gives
\[
 (B_{p,c}^{(s)})^2\leq
 \sum_{q}\frac1{q^2}
 +O\left(\frac{\ell_p}{cz}+\frac{\ell_p^2}{c^2}\right).
\]
After multiplication by $1/p$ and summation, the first terms cost
$O(L^3)$: with $M=pq$ and $A=(M+1)/4$, their sum is at most
$\sum_{M\leq X}\tau(A)/M\ll L^3$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:cubic}.
Mertens' estimates bound the errors by $O(L^2/z+L^2\log L)$, proving
(42.4).  Cauchy over the prescribed fibres gives the last assertion.
\end{proof}

For every fixed $B>3$, the endpoint also satisfies
\begin{equation}\tag{42.5}
 \sum_{z<p\leq L^B}p\sum_a(u^{\rm end}_{p,a})^2
 =O_B(\Lambda^2),
\end{equation}
and there are no endpoint incidences for $p>X/z$.  Indeed
Theorem~\ref{thm:soft-neighbor} gives
$t_p^{\rm end}\leq2A(b_p+u_p)$; uniformly for $p\leq L^B$,
$pb_p=O(\Lambda)$ and $pu_p=L^{o(1)}=o(\Lambda)$.  Residue Cauchy and
Mertens prove (42.5).  The super-polylogarithmic moving-$s$ range remains
nonempty and has harmonic length $\Theta(\log L)$.

\subsection{Kloosterman-fraction audit}
For an endpoint incidence put $m=4c^2s$ and aggregate its cofactors by
\begin{equation}\tag{45.1}
 G_{m,p}=\sum_{q}\frac{\kappa(pq)}q,
\end{equation}
where the sum includes every condition in (40.19); set it to zero unless
$m/4=c^2s$ with $s$ squarefree.  This decomposition is unique.  Additive
Parseval and (42.3) give the exact matrix identity
\begin{equation}\tag{45.2}
 \mathcal V_X^{\rm end}
 =\sum_{z<p\leq X/z}\sum_{h\bmod p}
   \left|\frac1p\sum_{m\geq1}G_{m,p}e_p(-h\bar m)\right|^2.
\end{equation}
The support has $(m,pq)=1$, $z<q\leq X/p$, and
$m\leq c(pq+1)<p(X+1)$.  The only valid reciprocity orientations are
\begin{equation}\tag{45.3}
 e_p(-h\bar m)=e_{pq}(-hq\,\bar m)
 =e_m(h\bar p)e\left(-\frac h{mp}\right).
\end{equation}
In particular, $q$ is never the phase modulus.

The DFI theorem, checked directly against the obtained 1997 article
\cite{DFI}, allows arbitrary \emph{separate} sequences $\alpha_m,\beta_n$
and gives
\begin{equation}\tag{45.4}
 \left|\sum_{m\sim M,n\sim N\atop(m,n)=1}
 \alpha_m\beta_ne(a\bar m/n)\right|
 \ll_\eps\|\alpha\|_2\|\beta\|_2
 (|a|+MN)^{3/8}(M+N)^{11/48+\eps}
\end{equation}
for every nonzero integer $a$, with no range hypothesis.  Its balanced
power-saving window is $N^{5/6+\eps}\ll M\ll N^{6/5-\eps}$.
Bettin--Chandee \cite{BettinChandee} similarly allow three separate
sequences and prove, for nonzero real $\vartheta$,
\begingroup
\footnotesize
\begin{equation}\tag{45.5}
 \begin{split}
 \left|\sum_{a\sim A,m\sim M,n\sim N\atop(m,n)=1}
 \nu_a\alpha_m\beta_ne(\vartheta a\bar m/n)\right|
 &\ll_\eps\|\nu\|_2\|\alpha\|_2\|\beta\|_2\\
 &\quad\times\left(1+\frac{|\vartheta|A}{MN}\right)^{1/2}\\
 &\quad\times\{(AMN)^{7/20+\eps}(M+N)^{1/4}
 +(AMN)^{3/8+\eps}(AN+AM)^{1/8}\}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
\endgroup
Neither statement allows the joint coefficient matrix $G_{m,p}$.  An exact
singular-value decomposition introduces its nuclear norm.  Worse, (45.2)
asks for the complete row-by-row $h$-norm: summing DFI over $h$ loses the
full number of frequencies, while dualizing for Bettin--Chandee introduces
an arbitrary $(h,p)$ matrix.  Their scalar savings do not pay these
projective-norm losses.  A long interval for $q$ does not help because $q$
stays inside $G_{m,p}$, and near $p=X/z$ that interval can be arbitrarily
short.  Thus the actual DFI PDF and the Bettin--Chandee theorem do not prove
(40.19); the missing input is an operator/large-sieve estimate for the joint
matrix, not a stronger scalar bilinear form.

\begin{theorem}[small-fibre moving-$s$ closure]\label{thm:small-fibre}
Let $\mathcal E(W_0)$ contain all endpoint incidences with
$m=4c^2s\leq W_0$.  If $4\leq W_0\leq z/2$, then
\begin{equation}\tag{45.6}
 \mathcal V(\mathcal E(W_0))
 =\sum_{z<p\leq X/z}\frac1p\sum_{4\leq m\leq W_0}G_{m,p}^2
 \ll W_0(L^3+L^2\log L).
\end{equation}
Consequently $W_0=\lfloor L^3/(\log L)^2\rfloor$ gives
$\mathcal V(\mathcal E(W_0))\ll\Lambda^2$ over the entire endpoint prime
range.  This is the injective prefix used by the wider closure below.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Here $m,m'<p$ and $|m-m'|<p$, so distinct supported cells are distinct
modulo $p$; inversion preserves distinctness.  Orthogonality of the full
$h$-sum in (45.2) therefore leaves no cross-cell terms and proves the
equality in (45.6).  For each unique decomposition $m=4c^2s$, the cells
with common $s$ lie in the fixed-$s$ family of
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-fibre}.  Its positive diagonal gives
\[
 \sum_p\frac1p\sum_m|G^{(s)}_{m,p}|^2
 \ll L^3+L^2\log L.
\]
There are at most $W_0/4$ possible squarefree $s$, which proves the upper
bound.  The specialization uses $W_0=o(z)$ and
$W_0(L^3+L^2\log L)\ll L^6/(\log L)^2=\Lambda^2$.
\end{proof}

The injective prefix is joined by a supported-cell argument.  If
$\mathscr G_C$ denotes cells $m=4c^2s$ with $c\geq C$, progression occupancy
of their cofactors gives $G_{4c^2s,p}\leq2/z+L/(2c)$ and hence
\[
 \mathcal D(\mathscr G_C)
 \ll\frac{L^4}{C\log z}+\frac{L^4}{z(\log z)^2}.
\]
A residue bucket modulo $p$ contains at most $T/p+1$ cells with $m\leq T$;
thus Cauchy gives
$\mathcal V(\mathscr A(T,C))\leq(1+T/z)\mathcal D(\mathscr G_C)$, where
$\mathscr A(T,C)=\{m\leq T,c\geq C\}$.
\begin{theorem}[prefix/wedge consequences of source Theorem 46.2]
\label{thm:endpoint-wedge}
Put
\begin{equation}\tag{46.8}
 W_1=\left\lfloor\frac{zL^2}{\log L}\right\rfloor
 \asymp\frac{L^5\log\log L}{(\log L)^2},
 \qquad W_2=\lfloor zW_1\rfloor.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\tag{46.10}
 \mathcal V(\mathscr A(W_1,1))=O(\Lambda^2),\qquad
 \mathcal V(\mathscr A(W_2,z))=O(\Lambda^2).
\end{equation}
Thus the full prefix through $W_1$ and the wedge $c\geq z$, $m\leq zW_1$
close over the complete endpoint-prime range.
\end{theorem}
Indeed the two substitutions in the preceding diagonal bound give (46.10).
Set
\[
 \mathscr A=\{m\leq W_1\}\cup\{c\geq z,\ m\leq W_2\},\qquad
 \mathscr B=\{m>W_1\}\cap(\{c<z\}\cup\{m>W_2\}).
\]
Then $\mathcal V(\mathscr A)=O(\Lambda^2)$, while the complete cell
diagonal is $o(\Lambda^2)$.  Positivity and the two-vector inequality give:
\begin{corollary}[exact endpoint remainder; source Corollary 46.3]
\label{cor:endpoint-core}
The endpoint target (40.19) is equivalent to
\begin{equation}\tag{46.13}\label{eq:endpoint-core}
 \boxed{\quad
 \sum_{z<p\leq X/z}\frac1p
 \sum_{\substack{m\ne m'\in\mathscr B_p\\m\equiv m'\ (p)}}
      G_{m,p}G_{m',p}=O(\Lambda^2).\quad}
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
Equation \eqref{eq:endpoint-core} is the current pair-level open core, not
all $m>W_0$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:nested-cube} refutes the literal raw
hierarchy (40.28) and raw-$H$ target (37.19), while
Theorem~\ref{thm:grid-obstruction} refutes the antichain hierarchy
\eqref{eq:antichain-wall} and the ordinary reduced-count moment target.
Neither refutation reaches (40.19), (37.27), $H_{\rm PF}'$, or the
conjecture.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Status.}
This is not a submission.  Both $3/4$ headlines remain
\textsc{claimed/provisional}, especially through Theorem~\ref{thm:pruned};
the internal proofs and refutations above do not close the open
critical-window ledger, \eqref{eq:endpoint-core}, $H_{\rm PF}'$, either
$H_{\rm WIN}$ window, or either supercritical crossing hypothesis.  The
count-below-one theorem is conditional, and the presently realized window
is below its thresholds.  The square-escape theorem and complete
linear/quadratic polynomial classification are proved, but they separate the
all-integer and polynomial-family escape mechanisms from the still-open
prime-survivor problem rather than proving a pointwise prime representation.
The all-degree cyclotomic obstruction is qualitative and ineffective;
$C_{\rm POLY}$ and repaired $C_{\rm SQ}'$ remain open.  The original
$C_{\rm SQ}$ is refuted: a computer-assisted theorem proves the three former
constant candidates have $W=+\infty$.  They are composite statistic-escapes
with explicit Egyptian-fraction representations, not Erd\H{o}s--Straus
counterexamples.  The complete census through $200{,}000$, and the optional
replay through $10^6$, finds exactly the squares and those three values, but
this proves no unbounded classification.  The proved hard-prime criterion and its exact ratio-spectrum
reformulation are terminating equivalences; the six small-$a$ laws, dual
fixed-$D$ taxonomy, and gcd cancellation cover are exact local structure.
The effective square-root law is weaker than the Section 51 tails on both
axes and is only almost-all coverage of the explicit $D=1$ layer.  None of
these proves the finite divisor list nonempty for every hard prime, a
pointwise bound, a fair-share estimate, or a new Erd\H{o}s--Straus theorem.
The enlarged twisted-square hunt covers only its exact coordinate boxes and
proves no unbounded classification or finiteness; only the right-to-left
inclusion in $C_{\rm SQ}'$ is proved.  The two-decade census is
Computational/INFO finite-range evidence.  Its absent post-$10^7$ record,
parity percentages, and normalized suprema imply no growth law and leave
$H_{\rm MOD}$ untouched.  The raw and antichain factorial-moment formulations are refuted
and that axis is dormant; the pair
endpoint and other possible pointwise minorants retain exactly the narrower
open scope stated above.
